MC	<p>e290_031c</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene E</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #5e6400;"><i>elephant</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene E</span> cells absorb excessive amounts of liquid, resulting in giant, swollen cells.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene H</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #5d48b8;"><i>hairy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene H</span> cells develop long, thread-like filaments that extend outward, creating a hairy, shaggy texture on the colony.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, eh, ++, eh"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct
MC	<p>7007_d35c</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #725b00;">Gene B</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #725b00;"><i>bubbly</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #725b00;">Gene B</span> produces excessive gas bubbles during growth, causing foamy appearance of the media.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene D</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #1b6397;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, bd, ++, bd"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect
MC	<p>16bf_5744</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene F</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #5e6400;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene F</span> colonies are covered in soft, fine filaments, giving them a fuzzy, cotton-like texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene Y</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #5d48b8;"><i>yolk</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene Y</span> cells develop a dense, yellowish core that resembles an egg yolk when viewed under a microscope.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, fy, ++, fy"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>3104_d617</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9d362f;">Gene W</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #9d362f;"><i>webbed</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9d362f;">Gene W</span> colonies produce delicate, web-like strands that connect neighboring colonies in a cobweb pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene X</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #1b6397;"><i>xenon</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene X</span> cells emit a faint glow under UV light, as if they were fluorescent.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, wx, ++, wx"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect
MC	<p>fffd_e32b</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #3b6c1f;">Gene C</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #3b6c1f;"><i>clumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #3b6c1f;">Gene C</span> grows in dense, irregular clusters, with cells clumping together rather than spreading smoothly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #923082;">Gene D</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #923082;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #923082;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, cd, ++, cd"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect
MC	<p>29cc_0a9d</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006b72;">Gene C</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006b72;"><i>clumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b72;">Gene C</span> grows in dense, irregular clusters, with cells clumping together rather than spreading smoothly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7e36a9;">Gene H</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #7e36a9;"><i>hairy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7e36a9;">Gene H</span> cells develop long, thread-like filaments that extend outward, creating a hairy, shaggy texture on the colony.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, ch, ++, ch"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct
MC	<p>786c_0f23</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #944308;">Gene B</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #944308;"><i>bubbly</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #944308;">Gene B</span> produces excessive gas bubbles during growth, causing foamy appearance of the media.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006b72;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006b72;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b72;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, br, ++, br"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>63c6_bd68</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene N</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #5e6400;"><i>nude</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene N</span> cells have an unusually smooth surface with no visible external features or textures.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #923082;">Gene Y</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #923082;"><i>yolk</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #923082;">Gene Y</span> cells develop a dense, yellowish core that resembles an egg yolk when viewed under a microscope.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, ny, ++, ny"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>3b4c_7e64</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene T</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #7f5317;"><i>toxic</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene T</span> secretes a toxic compound that inhibits or kills other microbial colonies nearby.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006d5f;">Gene X</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006d5f;"><i>xenon</i></span>' phenotype. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct
MC	<p>6b22_e4e6</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #725b00;">Gene C</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #725b00;"><i>clumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #725b00;">Gene C</span> grows in dense, irregular clusters, with cells clumping together rather than spreading smoothly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #923082;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #923082;"><i>jeweled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #923082;">Gene J</span> colonies appear dotted with tiny, iridescent spots that sparkle under light.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, cj, ++, cj"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>8566_c6ba</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006b72;">Gene J</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006b72;"><i>jeweled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b72;">Gene J</span> colonies appear dotted with tiny, iridescent spots that sparkle under light.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #9e2d58;">Gene X</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #9e2d58;"><i>xenon</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e2d58;">Gene X</span> cells emit a faint glow under UV light, as if they were fluorescent.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, jx, ++, jx"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect
MC	<p>185b_8152</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9d362f;">Gene J</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #9d362f;"><i>jeweled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9d362f;">Gene J</span> colonies appear dotted with tiny, iridescent spots that sparkle under light.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene R</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #1b6397;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>e412_0124</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006b72;">Gene A</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #006b72;"><i>amber</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b72;">Gene A</span> cells develop a rich yellow-orange pigmentation, giving the colony a warm, amber hue.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #923082;">Gene X</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #923082;"><i>xenon</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #923082;">Gene X</span> cells emit a faint glow under UV light, as if they were fluorescent.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, ax, ++, ax"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>5189_58e2</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene K</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #7f5317;"><i>knotted</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene K</span> cells grow in twisted, coiled shapes, resulting in a knotted or gnarled appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006b72;">Gene M</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006b72;"><i>militant</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b72;">Gene M</span> colonies are small, dense, and secrete compounds that inhibit the growth of nearby colonies.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, km, ++, km"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect
MC	<p>6492_3a76</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene D</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #5e6400;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5e6400;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene R</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #1b6397;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1b6397;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, dr, ++, dr"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect
MC	<p>1346_dab8</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006b72;">Gene E</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #006b72;"><i>elephant</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b72;">Gene E</span> cells absorb excessive amounts of liquid, resulting in giant, swollen cells.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #9e2d58;">Gene T</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #9e2d58;"><i>toxic</i></span>' phenotype. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>c0db_236f</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006d5f;">Gene F</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006d5f;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006d5f;">Gene F</span> colonies are covered in soft, fine filaments, giving them a fuzzy, cotton-like texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #9e2d58;">Gene K</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #9e2d58;"><i>knotted</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e2d58;">Gene K</span> cells grow in twisted, coiled shapes, resulting in a knotted or gnarled appearance.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, fk, ++, fk"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct
MC	<p>39fe_61d1</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene N</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #7f5317;"><i>nude</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene N</span> cells have an unusually smooth surface with no visible external features or textures.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7e36a9;">Gene Y</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #7e36a9;"><i>yolk</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7e36a9;">Gene Y</span> cells develop a dense, yellowish core that resembles an egg yolk when viewed under a microscope.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, ny, ++, ny"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct
MC	<p>9e53_ef66</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene R</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #7f5317;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f5317;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene X</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #5d48b8;"><i>xenon</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene X</span> cells emit a faint glow under UV light, as if they were fluorescent.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, rx, ++, rx"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>768e_0f23</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #266d48;">Gene D</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #266d48;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #266d48;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7e36a9;">Gene H</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #7e36a9;"><i>hairy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7e36a9;">Gene H</span> cells develop long, thread-like filaments that extend outward, creating a hairy, shaggy texture on the colony.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, dh, ++, dh"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect
MC	<p>d034_336b</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #3b6c1f;">Gene C</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #3b6c1f;"><i>clumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #3b6c1f;">Gene C</span> grows in dense, irregular clusters, with cells clumping together rather than spreading smoothly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene D</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #5d48b8;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #5d48b8;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, cd, ++, cd"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
MC	<p>adfc_d014</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #266d48;">Gene E</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #266d48;"><i>elephant</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #266d48;">Gene E</span> cells absorb excessive amounts of liquid, resulting in giant, swollen cells.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #3959ac;">Gene R</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #3959ac;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #3959ac;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, er, ++, er"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. unordered tetrads. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect
MC	<p>353c_a6ea</p> <h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #266d48;">Gene K</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #266d48;"><i>knotted</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #266d48;">Gene K</span> cells grow in twisted, coiled shapes, resulting in a knotted or gnarled appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #3959ac;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #3959ac;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #3959ac;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> </ul><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #202020; background: #ffffff;"><tr> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Set #</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Tetrad Genotypes</th> <th scope="col" align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;">Progeny<br/>Count</th></tr><tr> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><span style="font-size: medium;">1</span></td> <td align="center" style="border: 1px solid #888; padding: 4px 8px;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="ascus" width="156" height="120" viewBox="0 0 122 94" role="img" aria-label="Unordered ascus: ++, kr, ++, kr"><desc>Each recessive allele contributes its own color; plus alleles remain neutral. Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes. Instructions</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Find the row with the Parental Type for both genes.</li> <li>Step 2: Assign PD, NPD, TT for the other rows</li> <li>Step 3: Determine if the two genes are linked.</li> <ul><li>PD &gt;&gt; NPD &rarr; linked; PD &approx; NPD &rarr; unlinked</li></ul></ul><h6>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Determine Linkage</h6><p>The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> has unordered tetrads. A cross is made to study the linkage relationships among two genes.</p> <p>Using the table above, determine the linkage between the two genes.</p>	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> and on the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> chromosome.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #7B3F00;">BOTH</strong> <strong>linked</strong> AND <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #006400;">LINKED</strong> but are likely on <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Incorrect	The two genes are <strong style="color: #8B0000;">UNLINKED</strong> but may be on either the <strong style="color: #1E40AF;">SAME</strong> or <strong style="color: #D2691E;">DIFFERENT</strong> chromosomes.	Correct	The two genes are <strong style="color: #5B2C6F;">NEITHER</strong> <strong>linked</strong> NOR <strong>unlinked</strong>.	Incorrect
